BCT-HC: Application of Healthcare Technology Using Blockchain Technology Hyperledger Fabric



N. Nasurudeen Ahamed1, *, Tanweer Alam2
1 Department of Information Systems & Security, United Arab Emirates University, Al Ain, United Arab Emirates
2 Faculty of Computer & Information Systems, Islamic University of Madinah, Madinah, Saudi Arabia

Abstract

Blockchain innovation offers an information structure with built-in safeguards, including agreement, decentralization, and encryption, which ensure the accuracy of operations. It has broad use in a variety of fields, including smart factories, the Internet of Things (IoT), and healthcare. It is particularly relevant in the areas of healthcare information safety and privacy preservation. Digital healthcare records have been deployed faster because of communication technology, however, this has also increased risks to patient confidentiality, safety, and medical information. Another strategy to deal with the issue of medical data confidentiality and safety is the application of blockchain. Medical and health information includes treatment data gathered during patient care alongside private prevention of illness. Blockchain systems can be used in numerous capacities in healthcare organizations, including confidentiality and safety, to shield information about patients from unwanted access. Nevertheless, healthcare networks confront numerous security concerns, including connectivity, reliability, exchange of clinical information delivery, and patient deliberation, as a result of the inexperienced design of safety measures. Furthermore, deployment and information administration are the main issues for blockchain in healthcare due to the enormous amount of manufactured hardware gadgets. The platform is a communication mechanism that combines the use of computers with health records. It has been suggested that Hyperledger is the most developed collaborative chain technology. In contrast with various blockchain platforms, Hyperledger concentrates on developing enterprise-level standardized implementations. The Hyperledger framework is used for sensitive data in digital welfare, but neither restricted access nor thorough permission was taken into account. It is simple to determine fraudulent data collection by a malevolent person via confirmation of data by additional subjects, and the original, unmodified information can be recovered. Data can only be influenced if a bad person has access to over 50% of the blockchain network's nodes through security breaches, which is nearly unattainable. As a result, blockchain technology can stop information from being faked or falsified, improving the data's durability and dependability.

Keywords: Blockchain, Decentralization, Digital healthcare, Encryption, Healthcare, Hyperledger, Hyperledger fabric, Internet of Things, Smart factory.

* Corresponding author N. Nasurudeen Ahamed: Department of Information Systems & Security, United Arab Emirates University, Al Ain, United Arab Emirates; E-mail: nasurmecse@gmail.com

INTRODUCTION

Blockchain is essentially a distributed, decentralized electronic record that keeps track of activities safely and irreversibly. Blockchain innovation [1] operates on a network of peers, guaranteeing that no individual has total control over the data. This reduces the chance of information being altered or illicit access while boosting transparency. This feature is especially helpful in the field of healthcare, where patient confidentiality and security of information are crucial. The permanent nature of blockchain files adds a critical degree of safety for health information. Since every item or transaction on the blockchain is cryptographically linked to every other deal, amending previous data would need modifying each transaction that comes afterward. This unchangeable feature safeguards sensitive knowledge and health data, significantly reducing the likelihood of forgery and increasing the trust of patients. A full file of transactions is accessible to all participants in the network, facilitating regulatory adherence, product confirmation, and health information monitoring. The integration of computer science into healthcare [2, 3] gave rise to healthcare technology, which greatly enhanced healthcare. Blockchain computing has several uses in the medical field outside of patient information. These include medication safety, managing supply chains, sickness forecasting, medication accountability, claims processing, and others. The easy-to-use system and reliable environment of blockchain technology yield useful outcomes that increase parties' faith. The Hyperledger Fabric system (individual Blockchain) safeguards patient confidentiality and the integrity of their health information.

An Overview of Blockchain Technology and its Version

Blockchain is a decentralized technology that allows several parties to conduct operations and keep track of them in a record. It is based on a dispersed network of nodes that securely and irrevocably log and confirm events. Every node in the network confirms every payment as it gets recorded to the blockchain, and its record is copied among all of the nodes [3-5]. Blockchain has two types: one is permission, and another is permissionless. The example is listed as follows:

• Permissioned Blockchain: Hyperledger Fabric.

• Permissionless Blockchain: Ethereum.

Since its establishment, from the initial digital currency to the present blockchain-based deployment for industry, blockchain technology has been utilized by numerous sectors as a component of the infrastructure of various enterprises that demand openness, honesty, and accuracy in Industry 5.0. Blockchain technology has advanced (Table 1) from version 1.0 to version 5.0, making it more trustworthy and appropriate for use in commercial settings and industrial uses.

Table 1 Blockchain Technology Advancements.
Version Description
Blockchain 1.0 The innovative digital currency system was driven by the configurable currency known as the digital currency bitcoin. The virtual currency's transfer architecture is decentralized and key-based, which is how the blockchain system got its beginnings.
Blockchain 2.0 Blockchain-based programs have become prevalent in social domains like banking, transactions among peers, data legitimate enrollment, property and trademark approval, and smart leadership. These applications are based on the expandable culture.
Blockchain 3.0 It expands the use of blockchain technology for decentralized apps (DApps), enhancing productivity and trust among people through decentralization, non-tampering, and reliable collaboration.
Blockchain 4.0 An advancement over the prior version that uses the consensus protocol to regulate network behavior and increase the viability of DApps for real-time business situations applicable in the industrial age (IA) 4.0.
Blockchain 5.0 The current version of the blockchain is regarded as an emerging generation due to eliminating conventional blockchain constraints and using simulated links for quicker execution and more protection.

Blockchain versions 1.0, 2.0, 3.0, 4.0, and 5.0 are in distinct deployment phases. They are all concurrent perspectives of growth, ranging from 1.0 to 5.0, and they have proper roles in various industries. Fig. (1) shows the extent of blockchain innovation advancement.

Fig. (1))
The range of blockchain technology advancements.

Blockchain's Characteristics

• Peer-to-peer Connection Unchanging Distributed Record: Make sure that the blockchain's data structure maintains the structural permanence of the single node record.

• Privacy methods, like encryption, hash computations, and digital signatures, ensure the confidentiality and safety of operations.

• Agreement Techniques: Utilizing technology to guarantee that consensus is reached, this pure logical mechanism for blockchain communal authentication builds confidence amongst all participants.

• Intelligent Agreement: It is a guaranteed reliable enterprise that conducts operations with no intervention from outside parties. Intelligent agreements are mainly employed to lower transaction expenses and offer safeguards for other contracts. As a result, intelligent contracts guarantee the legitimacy and dependability of each exchange that occurs between nodes.

• Being Transparent and Exchange: Everyone with an equal right to access and use blockchain data can use its features.

• The Accord:Certain nodes agree to finish checking and validate operations in just a portion of the time. The network's consensus is taken seriously if multiple nodes can agree without having to share assets in the deal.

• Competitive Rivalry: Algorithms compute the tasks of every node and establish accounting entitlements.

• Integrity and Reliability: Each document is meticulously and accurately produced while being closely monitored.

• Reliable and Dependable: Intricate checksum sharing ensures honesty, accessibility, and privacy; data encryption and cryptography techniques guard against data manipulation and forgery. An encryption method (digital signature) that requires every link to have its key and only transmits packets when the key is in an appropriate state allows for the detection of numerous hackers.

HYPERLEDGER FRAMEWORK

Hyperledger

The Ubuntu Foundation launched the Hyperledger initiative in the last month of 2015 as a collection of open-source blockchains and associated tools to facilitate the cooperative creation of distributed records based on blockchain technology [6, 7].

Hyperledger's “cover approach” fosters and advances a variety of enterprise blockchain structures, uses, archives, interactions, and technology. At present, Hyperledger is the home of the subsequent foundation developments.

• Hyperledger Fabric

• Hyperledger Sawtooth

• Hyperledger Iroha

• Hyperledger Indy

• Hyperledger Burrow

Among the above-mentioned hyperledger frameworks, we will discuss only one framework (i.e., Hyperledger Fabric).

HyperLedger Fabric (HLF)

One of the Hyperledger initiatives run by the Ubuntu Foundation is the blockchain architecture deployment known as Hyperledger Fabric. Fabric stands out as a permissioned network system where users have verified credentials. Its flexible design divides the handling of transactions into three phases, namely ordering, transaction validation and commitment, and decentralized reasoning execution and consensus (also known as “chain code”).

Using widely utilized and tested technology, Hyperledger Fabric is a distributed database for intelligent contracts with an extensible structure that enables adaptable solutions for various services [8-10]. Using a Byzantine Fault Tolerance agreement system, confirming peers run a duplicated state system that takes three different kinds of events as actions. Because the fabric uses a permissioned record, it has an encryption structure that allows permission and identification. Public key credentials enable registration and trade permission, while in-band cryptography guarantees chain code integrity. It adopts a unique reaching-an-agreement strategy that preserves confidentiality while enabling growth.

Before getting able to see the general design, one must comprehend the typical Hyperledger Fabric network pieces, as well as the ones that are exclusive to the suggested solution.

  • Peer: Associated with the points that comprise the system's structures. They are in charge of dispersing and keeping all of the data. These are the parts that provide the system's orderer nodes with knowledge so they can set up the processed units.
  • Orderer: One of the most crucial components of the network is the orderer. It is overseen by peers. They must come to the necessary agreement whether they are a part of one or more groups. They are involved in each exchange about the network setting. Additionally, systems use simple security measures for channels, limiting who can configure them as well as access and modify information to channels.
  • Channel: A channel is a way for people on the network to interact with one another. In this instance, it is an encrypted communication method that enables secrecy and information separation. This level is in charge of maintaining data protection and data transfer among network clients.
  • Consensus: The distributed ledger's present way of verifying blocks. There have been three distinct algorithms for the Fabric architecture over the years. At first, a method called Solo was created, enabling the presence of just one orderer network responsible for processing the blocks it creates. Simultaneously, a different algorithm, known as the Kafka algorithm, was developed. This algorithm is Byzantine fault-tolerant and only needs one node to handle every transaction. Over time, this approach made setup and upkeep more difficult. In light of these two, Raft's agreement emerges.
  • Certification Authorities (CA): They are equivalent to a common component of public key infrastructures(PKIs), which are in charge of distributing electronic certificates. The purpose of this tier is to verify that those involved or parties in the interaction are who they claim to be. To confirm that an internet site is trustworthy, credible CAs typically give electronic certificates on their behalf.
  • Membership Service Provider (MSP): It gathers all of the encryption methods applied to online communication. Every company needs an MSP, which houses its cryptographic data, including its secret keys and the certificate authority (CA) that granted it. Peers use this information to verify the outcomes of transaction processing (recommendations), while users use it to verify their activities.
  • Chain Code: In essence, chain code, also known as an intelligent contract, is the company's code that controls how different people or businesses in a blockchain network communicate or conduct business with one another. Chaincode calls produce collections and get the record or global state. It is an element of a program that is installed on other nodes in the context of Hyperledger Fabric connections to allow communication with the common ledger of those networks. Chaincode is separate from the validating peer process and operates in a safe Docker environment. Chaincode uses events that are uploaded by services to set up and preserve the state of the blockchain.

• Put into practice in the Go/Java/JavaScript language.

• Combined With Connections in a Module.

• The evaluating peer operates in a container using Docker.

Orderer and Pluggable Ordering Service

In Hyperledger Fabric, an orderer's job is to group events in a block and organize them sequentially. Orderers are also in charge of allowing the establishment of networks [11-14]. Three different ordering setup types are available in Hyperledger Fabric.

  • Solo.
  • Kafka.
  • Raft.

Solo

As the name implies, the solo ordering system comprises just one node that handles everything (Fig. 2). It is not fault-tolerant hence it is not advised for true use in production. It is advised for proof of idea development or debugging.

Fig. (2))
Solo orderer for instance.

Raft

A leader and the other nodes make up the raft ordering system. This network's controller is chosen by choice, following a reaching an agreement process. Supporters imitate the leader's behavior. Compared to Kafka, this ordering mechanism is significantly simpler to set up (Fig. 3).

Fig. (3))
Raft ordering assistance for instance.

Kafka

An additional “the ruler and supporters” based system for ordering is built on Apache Kafka. Zookeepers are also needed to oversee the ordering networks. It has been accessible since Hyperledger Fabric v1.0. Still, it was difficult to adopt once Raft was released due to its greater administration costs and the need for complicated settings to be used with Hyperledger Fabric (Fig. 4).

Fig. (4))
Kafka ordering assistance for instance.

State Repositories

CouchDB and LevelDB may both be utilized as state archives thanks to Hyperledger Fabric. The two databases listed above are open source, NoSQL-based, and cannot handle SQL queries or the relational database design. These two facilitate the fundamental functions of chain code, including retrieving a key-value conjunction, storing it, and doing queries using the keys. CouchDB, nevertheless, is recommended since it can execute sophisticated, complex searches. NoSQL databases provide an enormous number of options for managing information.

Table 2 describes the difference between SQL and NoSQL databases.

Table 2 Comparison Between SQL and NoSQL Database.
SQL Database NoSQL Database
Relational Database Management System(RDBMS) Distributed Database
Schema Oriented Schema Less
ACID(Atomicity,Consistency,Isolation,Durability)Property BASE (Basically, Available, Soft State, Eventual Consistency) Property
Vertical Scaling Horizontal Scaling
API Not Supported API Supported

In SQL database, there are complex queries that can becompared to those in the NoSQL database. Moreover, NoSQL is an open source, and it provides many applications, So, all medical-oriented data are stored in NoSQL databases. The types of NoSQL databases are as follows:

• Key/Value Database.

• Colum-oriented Database.

• Document-oriented Database.

• Graph-riented Database.

• Wide Row Database.

The concept of organizations with distinct rights and privacy practices for each organization is offered by Hyperledger. Additionally, company solutions call for the development of complicated data structures and industrial logic, which Hyperledger Fabric enables through the use of smart contracts [15-17], occasionally referred to as chain code.

The various kinds and operational details of each chain code are listed below:

• Lifecycle System Chaincode (LSCC): It is utilized in a hyper ledger fabric network for chain code setup, implementation, and refreshes.

• Configuration System Chaincode (CSCC): It is employed to manage peer-level network environments.

• Query System Chaincode (QSCC): It is employed to manage query registry APIs, such as retrieving transactions and blocks.

CouchDB is Used to Connect Two Organizations

Two organizations, org1 and org2, with a pair of peers each, make up this network. While peer1 in both organizations is limited to committers, peer0 in both has installed chain code and is a supporter. Every organization has the right to certify people. CouchDB is the database of preference. The efficiency of a lone orderer will also be simulated using this network (Fig. 6).

BENEFITS OF BLOCKCHAIN IN HEALTHCARE

Blockchain is regarded as a versatile platform that applies to several industries, the most notable of which is healthcare. Because of the broad range of issues that can be resolved by the features and attributes provided by blockchain, the medical sector is vital and presents several prospects for using this concept. Decentralization is a key component of blockchain technology that is advantageous for applications in the healthcare sector [18-21]. It enables dispersed application deployment without depending on a single central entity. Patients and other healthcare users can be informed about how, by whom, and when their information is being utilized because of the openness and honesty created by the blockchain's data replication across every network node. Because of the way it works, it can shield information from grief, fraud, or safety hazards. Additionally, because of its inviolability, nothing that has been linked to the chain can be changed or altered, guaranteeing the reliability and accuracy of patient files. It is ensured that only individuals with authorized access privileges can decode the data by using encryption methods that secure it, enhancing its confidentiality and safety. Additionally, because digital keys are being used and the patients on the blockchain have false identities, data about them can be exchanged across partners without revealing their recognition.

Fig. (6))
CouchDB is used to connect two organizations.

Leveraging Hyperledger Fabric for Digital Wellness Data (DWD)

Digital wellness data (DWD) is a type of medical records system that is utilized to handle, exchange, and store clinical information concerning patients. These networks have grown significantly in the past few decades and have long been a significant aspect of the healthcare sector [22]. This is mostly because of the computerization of medical structures, which has made it more necessary than ever to gather and distribute enormous quantities of information. DWD has been employed to raise the standard of healthcare for patients by giving medical professionals timely knowledge of critical health data. It provides various benefits to the field of health research besides helping handle data for medical examination and therapy.

Blockchain technology provides numerous benefits for safeguarding the confidentiality of patients in DWD.

• Better Safety for Information (BSI): By keeping healthcare data in an interconnected system that is impervious to outside abuse, the blockchain provides greater safety.

• Greater Openness (GO): People can keep an eye on how their information is being utilized thanks to the blockchain, which assists in avoiding data abuse.

• More Seclusion (MS): Individuals can manage who has possession of their information thanks to the blockchain. This may lessen the likelihood of hacking and abuse.

When it comes to highly confidential information like electronic health records, blockchain offers perks. Blockchain technology protects and monitors transmissions of information through encryption, making it hard to change or falsify information without being caught. Because blockchain is distributed, there is not one single source of collapse, which lowers the possibility of hacks and information thefts.

Powered by Blockchain Healthcare Deployment

Healthcare and wellness information includes clinical information gathered during medical treatment along with private prevention of illness. The platform is a collaboration platform that combines technological advances with health information. Three elements make up the framework: data assistance, the privacy of data, and data acquisition. The safeguarding component creates a safeguard for health assistance, the data gathering module gathers health data about patients, and the service function fulfills patient demands for health records.

A Blockchain System for Preserving Medical Data

Blockchain contributes to the development of decentralized applications and data-sharing systems. Medical management of data has historically been a computerized system that operates autonomously. The disadvantage of this method of leadership is that it is challenging to establish the true exchange of information due to the overly centralized power of data administration. Shared text features are introduced by blockchain technology. Because several organizations together preserve and oversee the file contents entered in blockchain technology, openness and honesty of data details are guaranteed by the shared oversight by various divisions, which also establishes the transparency and accountability of rules controlling blockchain technology deals. This will essentially address the issues associated with conventional healthcare data administration, such as inadequate work productivity and an overly disorganized atmosphere at work.

Blockchain can also be used to create a reliable transfer mechanism. The simplest and most fundamental procedures of adding, removing, altering, and inquiring constitute the entirety of healthcare records data administration. Nevertheless, blockchain eliminates the two fundamental steps in the management of preserved knowledge deletion and modification and streamlines the handling of preserved data. The technical architecture of blockchain ensures the safety and immutability of data. Each data block in the blockchain further keeps track of the hash algorithm and genesis time of the prior block. This time-marked network architecture increases the rate at which health data is used and makes standard inspection, monitoring, and accountability easier.

Lastly, blockchain can strengthen assignment norms and data exchange. The automation of exchanging historical data can be optimized through the utilization of block link technology and smart agreements. The intelligent agreement is irreversible once it is put into action and is not affected by outside processes. This functionality can be used by hospitals to consolidate stake allocation policies. Smart agreements have the potential to significantly increase the value of healthcare data by encouraging involvement from stakeholders in sharing data, increasing effectiveness and the speed of data trade, and changing member activity. The integrity of healthcare data is guaranteed and the hidden container operation associated with the conventional sharing of data is restrained in this mandatory exchange of data.

Information Administration in Healthcare Utilizing Blockchain

One of the greatest current advances in computer science is the use of blockchain technology, which enables network members to keep track of activities and quickly exchange them with other users of the blockchain. Four basic groups were put forth in this field to address privacy and security concerns: First, methods for encryption of data and decoding. Second, a fresh approach to electronic identities. The secure transmission of information technique comes in third. The blockchain-based key-generating process comes in at number four.

The above sections talk about the various repositories (CouchDB and LevelDB) in the Hyperledger Fabric. Table 2 describes the contrast between the CouchDB and Level DB. In contrast, among the two databases, CouchDB shows the best parameters compared to LevelDB. CouchDB mechanism records every update, much like a blockchain. CouchDb is also called a document-oriented database. It stores and retrieves data as a key value pair, but the value part is stored as a document. The document is stored in (JavaScript Object Notation) JSON formats. Example: Patient Data stored in Document DB format. Fig. (7) talks about creating the patient document data in the couch database.

Blockchain technology may be applied to every facet of medical facilities, including economics, management, oversight, data, inspection, and historical records. It can also offer crucial technical support for reconstructing hospital operations and informatization.

In this effort, a confidential blockchain network using rights was constructed utilizing the Hyperledger Fabric platform to archive hospital records in a secure and unchangeable manner for future study and information access as required.

Zero Knowledge Proof (ZKF)

In the description, the blockchain has a high degree of transparency. All of the data entered into the record is accessible to any node in the Bitcoin system, and the zero-knowledge-proof technique is incorporated into the blockchain to protect the anonymity of individuals and businesses.

INTERPLANETARY FILE SYSTEM (IPFS)

A network-wide exchange mechanism called IPFS was created to enable dispersed, durable transfer of files and archiving. Like the web, the interplanetary file system (IPFS) is a decentralized peer-to-peer data transmission network. The primary distinction is that, whereas geographically based routing is used on the web, document-based indexing was utilized by IPFS. Since the files cannot be manipulated, data using this document-based referencing is immutable. A novel file is formed when an item is modified. In this peer-to-peer system, files are hashed to establish trust. This trust can be verified by rebuilding the hash algorithm or Merkletree from the unverified material. Massive documents can be chopped using IPFS, which is capable of downloading multiple parts simultaneously as needed. Distributed, global network architecture: By storing substantial volumes of multimedia information in IPFS, this type of network layout can address memory restrictions in the blockchain. Retention of encoded records: IPFS tacks on a hash function to the secret data that is exclusive of electronic data. This code ensures that the associated document cannot be altered. The hash and the file match exactly.

Each document that is uploaded to an IPFS cluster is assigned a distinct hash value that is determined by the information inside the item. When an archive is asked to be obtained, IPFS (Fig. 9) uses its hash table to identify its location and then retrieves its contents. Blockchain and IPFS together may be a useful way to address the blockchain capacity dilemma.

Fig. (9))
Storing medical records in decentralized storage through Hyperledger Fabric.

Both patients and physicians gain entry to healthcare information by participating in the solution's permission process. Intelligent agreements can be established or updated by administrator clients, who are also in charge of monitoring the blockchain.

CHALLENGES AND LIMITATIONS

In the healthcare industry, blockchain technology's potential to enhance the handling of data, confidentiality, and privacy [23] has garnered a lot of interest. Still, there are several barriers to its adoption.

• Threats to the Safety and Confidentiality of Data: Broadband capacity and processing power.

• Exposed Information: Complicated pharmaceutical tracking scenario.

• Continuous Surveillance of the Safeguards for Health Information: Lag in time while checking the blocks.

• Data Manipulation and Entry Restriction: Insufficient time to process.

• Information Protection: Difficult to identify gadget signs.

• Clinical Information is Handled and Tracked: Insufficient connectivity between the gadgets and the data center.

Since HLF is a proprietary blockchain technological advance and no production-grade chain codes were available as open source, we were unwilling to gather data with varying difficulties in performance according to intricacy. Instead, we had to model the amount of code by employing different computations. Low RAM (Random Access Memory) was one of the equipment traits that posed a major obstacle.

FUTURE OPPORTUNITIES

The world of mankind will be greatly influenced by blockchain technology, and blockchain healthcare has yet to fully realize its promise. Blockchain healthcare will shake up humankind's current order, recognition by law, and financial structures in addition to revolutionizing technology and transforming entire sectors. The following development trends are anticipated to emerge as blockchain technology continues to mature.

Intelligent Computation

Blockchain innovation can be used in intelligent systems to offer a shared environment and decentralized trade for different intelligence elements, such as information, computations, and computing capacity. This could lead to the development of artificial intelligence and innovative design in a whole new direction. Blockchain will increase openness, simplicity, and decision-reliability in the healthcare sector. Since all blockchain data is accessible to everyone, artificial intelligence will be the cornerstone for confidentiality security and fighting counterfeiting.

Improve IoT Data Safety and Openness

Blockchain innovation in the healthcare industry has the potential to improve IoT data safety and openness while also promoting future IoT flexibility, effectiveness, and uniformity. Blockchain-enabled Internet of Things (IoT) devices used in healthcare will allow patients to manage who gets access to the data they gather, enhancing the gadget's protection against attackers and giving an accurate track of who has looked at the information. Supply chain transactions may be carried out via blockchain-based methods backed by intelligent agreements, depending on the specific conditions monitored by connected devices.

Privacy in Healthcare

Encrypted medical records that are only accessible by certain individuals can be stored on blockchain. Healthcare involves additional criteria, like data exchange and connectivity, for safeguarding patient data. Interchange is the method of exchanging information with different sources. With the involvement of insurance companies, authorities, medical professionals, and patients, distributed ledgers offer safe and private healthcare administration. Blockchain can be used for a variety of tasks in the medical field. Blockchain technology helps doctors find genetic codes by controlling the pharmaceutical manufacturing process, enabling secure transmission of medical data from patients, and simplifying the exchange of information about patients. Keeping patient health information safe from hackers and ensuring confidentiality through verified access is one of the biggest problems the healthcare sector is now facing. Healthcare growth is built on blockchain security; in the future, blockchain security will primarily develop through technological application, application deepening, and supervisory systems.

REAL-WORLD EXAMPLES OF SUCCESSFUL BLOCKCHAIN IMPLEMENTATIONS IN HEALTHCARE

The potential of blockchain technology to revolutionize the healthcare sector through increased data security, more openness, and streamlined procedures is demonstrated by several successful blockchain applications in the field.

I. MedRec was developed by MIT Media Lab, a blockchain-based electronic medical record system designed to manage authentication, confidentiality, and data sharing. This system allows patients to control their medical records and provides a transparent audit trail, ensuring data integrity and reducing administrative efforts [24].

II. Patientory is a blockchain-based solution that focuses on improving data interoperability and patient care management. By storing health information on a blockchain, Patientory enables secure and seamless data sharing among healthcare providers, which can lead to more coordinated and effective patient care [25].

III. Pharmaceutical Supply Chain: Blockchain technology is being utilized to secure the pharmaceutical supply chain. Companies like chronicled have implemented blockchain to track pharmaceuticals from production to the end consumer, ensuring authenticity and reducing the risk of counterfeit drugs entering the supply chain. This system increases transparency and trust among stakeholders [26].

IV. Estonian Health Record System employs blockchain to secure patient health records. The system ensures data integrity and provides an immutable audit trail of all access and modifications to health records, significantly enhancing data security and patient trust [27].

V. Patientdatachain platform uses blockchain to create a decentralized database for medical records, enabling patients to grant access to their records to doctors, hospitals, and labs securely. Patientdatachain ensures that data is accurate, up-to- date, and accessible, thus improving patient care and operational efficiency in healthcare settings [28].

The above examples demonstrate that blockchain can address significant challenges in healthcare, such as data security, interoperability, and supply chain integrity, leading to more efficient and reliable healthcare systems. All the acronyms used in this manuscript are listed in Table 4.

CONCLUSION

Blockchain is decentralized, open, and immutable, and it has the potential to revolutionize healthcare by facilitating secure storage of information, connectivity, and focus on patient treatment. There are many possibilities for this technology, such as performing clinical research and healthcare information, preventing deception, and enhancing the oversight of supply chains (Drugs). The application of blockchain technology to health management of data intends to lower expenses, streamline activities, enhance the health of patients, and do away with middlemen. To advance information systems in hospitals reform and enhance the safety of healthcare information exchange and preservation, Hyperledger technology is being applied in the healthcare sector. Hyperledger is the most practical and user-friendly convey to be utilized when carrying out a corporate cross-industry blockchain solution because it provides variable management of the parameters mentioned earlier within a network on an element level, allowing safety to be implemented in various manners within a single network. A flexible design to build industrial blockchain apps is offered by Hyperledger Fabric. Because Hyperledger Fabric has made specific parts, including consensus processes and membership offerings, the structure is now easily expandable and preserved, rendering it perfect for a variety of uses in industry scenarios. Although this is merely a transitory phase, we ought to worry about resolving the information capacity issue from the blockchain in the future. IPFS and blockchain can help reduce the archive strain of blockchain.

Impact of Blockchain-Enabled IoT Applications for Smart Agriculture and Healthcare to Promote Sustainable Economic Growth and Smart Health Management Ecosystem in Industry 5.0



Bhavneet Kaur Sachdev1, Sumanta Bhattacharya2, *
1 Calcutta University, Kolkata, India
2 Maulana Abul Kalam Azad University of Technology, Kolkata, West Bengal, India

Abstract

Within the framework of Industry 5.0, the incorporation of blockchain-enabled IoT applications into smart agriculture and healthcare has significant consequences for long-term economic growth and the creation of ecosystems for smart health management. The environmental, economic, industrial, and stakeholder implications of smart agriculture's transparent supply chains, which are made possible by blockchain technology and precision farming techniques, lessen the environmental impact of conventional farming by cutting down on resource use. Blockchain technology and Internet of Things (IoT) devices safeguard patient records, allowing for more eco-friendly procedures with less paper waste. These innovations maximize the use of agricultural resources, which boosts output while decreasing expenditures from an economic perspective. Better patient outcomes and lower healthcare costs are possible because of blockchain technology's assurance of data integrity and interoperability. This promotes a fairer and more inclusive economic climate, giving smaller players a voice. Innovation and integration are propelled by the industrial sector. By fostering an environment of open communication and cooperation, the tenets of Industry 5.0 aim to bring together many sectors of the economy to improve technological standards, strengthen cybersecurity, and standardize procedures. Awareness, training, and community participation are all made easier by stakeholders, which include NGOs. For these technologies to be adopted in an ethical and responsible manner, their advocacy for legislation that supports them and the protection of data ownership rights are vital. In the future, there will be more international cooperation, new technological solutions to problems with energy efficiency, and the creation of universal benchmarks.

Keywords: Agricultural resources, Advocacy, Blockchain, Cybersecurity, Community participation, Data integrity, Ecosystem, Eco-friendly, Economic perspective, Environment impact, Healthcare cost, Industry 5.0, International cooperation, Internet of Things, Paper waste, NGOs, Smart health management, Stakeholders, Smart agriculture, Technology.

* Corresponding author Sumanta Bhattacharya: Maulana Abul Kalam Azad University of Technology, Kolkata, West Bengal, India; E-mail: sumanta.21394@gmail.com

INTRODUCTION

Blockchain-enabled Internet of Things (IoT) applications have the potential to revolutionise smart agriculture and healthcare. Blockchain and Internet of Things applications can improve supply chain visibility and auditability in the context of smart agriculture. Data from Internet of Things (IoT) sensors, such as those tracking soil moisture, temperature, humidity, and crop health, can be recorded and stored securely on a distributed ledger, giving stakeholders confidence in the reliability of data pertaining to agricultural practises and products. This not only promotes sustainable and ethical agricultural practises but also minimises the possibility of fraud by providing consumers with verified information about the origin and quality of agricultural products. Optimising resource utilisation is one way in which the adoption of blockchain and IoT in agriculture promotes long-term economic prosperity [1]. To guarantee that farmers are fairly compensated for their goods, smart contracts on the blockchain can streamline transactions and legal agreements. Increased agricultural output and profits may result from the greater ease and openness of financial dealings. Moreover, the information gathered by IoT devices may be used for data-driven decision-making, allowing farmers to implement precision agriculture practises that boost productivity while reducing environmental impact. A smart health management ecosystem can be established when blockchain technology is combined with the Internet of Things. Patient data obtained through IoT devices, such as wearable health monitors, can be securely kept on a blockchain. The confidentiality of patient information is protected while open communication between medical professionals is facilitated. Increased patient agency over their own health information supports care delivery centered on the individual. Automating procedures like insurance claims with blockchain-based smart contracts can cut down on paperwork and boost the healthcare system's productivity. Human-centered design and cutting-edge tech go hand in hand in the “Industry 5.0” paradigm shift. In this light, IoT applications enabled by blockchain are congruent with the goal of establishing interdependent ecosystems that put an emphasis on efficiency and sustainability. Blockchain transactions are transparent and safe, so everyone involved in agriculture and healthcare can trust the data being shared between their connected equipment [2]. Industry 5.0 is characterised by the rise of cooperative, networked systems that are capable of enhancing themselves. Blockchain's increased security and privacy is a major selling point when applied to the Internet of Things. Sensitive data is generated and transferred in both smart agriculture and healthcare. The distributed ledger and cryptographic properties of blockchain technology guarantee that information cannot be altered and that only authorised users can access it. This not only promotes good data management practises but also protects the privacy of individuals' health information throughout the agriculture supply chain. It has a significant impact on smart agriculture and healthcare, fostering the growth of sustainable economies and new smart health management ecosystems [3]. As Industry 5.0 develops further, these technologies provide novel answers for the administration of agricultural operations and healthcare systems, with an emphasis on efficiency, transparency, and security.

Environment Consideration

There are major environmental issues with smart agricultural and healthcare systems that include blockchain and the IoT. In order to solve environmental problems, technical progress in these areas must be in line with sustainability objectives, making these factors critical. The Internet of Things (IoT) allows for the improvement of resource usage in smart agriculture through the use of devices like sensors, drones, and automated machinery. Because of this, farmers are able to use less water, fertiliser, and pesticide, leading to more efficient practices overall. This helps with sustainable farming and resource conservation by reducing the negative impact of conventional farming practices on the environment. The use of blockchain technology in smart agriculture improves eco-friendliness by creating a verifiable and transparent supply chain. By being open and honest about where their food comes from, consumers can rest certain that their food is not part of any unlawful or unsustainable methods that harm the environment [4]. With this information at their fingertips, shoppers can demand more sustainable agricultural techniques that are good for the environment. Blockchain and the Internet of Things (IoT) can also simplify healthcare operations, which in turn reduces the need for resources and the amount of waste produced. There will be less need for paper records thanks to digital health records kept on blockchain systems, which means less paper waste and less pollution. Wearable monitors and remote patient monitoring tools are examples of Internet of Things (IoT) gadgets that are finding use in healthcare. These devices allow for early detection and preventative interventions, which in turn reduce the environmental effect of emergency treatments and hospitalisations. By reducing energy waste and making better use of healthcare resources, this new proactive approach is environmentally friendly [5]. Health data stored on blockchain systems is decentralised and secure, which means less need for physical infrastructure like huge data centres. This means less energy use and a smaller carbon impact. With more and more healthcare systems turning to digital solutions, this is becoming more important, especially because people are starting to worry about the effects of data storage on the environment. If blockchain and IoT are serious about reducing their negative effects on the environment, they must be designed to use less energy. To make sure that the advantages of these technologies do not come at the expense of increasing energy consumption and carbon emissions, innovations in low-power IoT devices and consensus procedures in blockchain networks are crucial. Another important factor to think about is how to properly dispose of the electrical garbage that is produced by old Internet of Things devices. To lessen the toll that electronic waste has on the environment and to forestall contamination from toxic substances, producers should make recycling and responsible disposal their top priorities [6]. Thorough environmental impact studies should precede the use of blockchain and IoT technologies in order to detect and mitigate any adverse effects. By taking this preventative measure, we can make sure that these technologies have the most positive impact on smart agriculture and healthcare while having the least negative impact on the environment. There must be a concerted effort to inform the public of the environmental advantages of blockchain and the Internet of Things (IoT) in smart farming and healthcare. Showcasing the beneficial environmental effect of these technologies and encouraging stakeholders to adopt sustainable behaviors may promote a collective commitment to ecologically responsible use. A firm dedication to environmental sustainability should govern the deployment of blockchain and the Internet of Things, notwithstanding the revolutionary potential they hold in smart healthcare and agriculture. If we want our technical innovations to have a beneficial and long-lasting effect on the environment, we must find a way to balance them [7]. Fig. (1) explains the impact of blockchain and IoT on environment.

Fig. (1))
Impact of Blockchain and IoT on the Environment.

Blockchain and IoT Contribution to Economic Growth

In the context of Industry 5.0, blockchain-enabled IoT applications might revolutionise smart agricultural and healthcare systems, which in turn could create significant economic development. By maximising efficiency and savviness with resources through the provision of real-time data on crop conditions, soil health, and weather patterns, blockchain and IoT technologies can improve the efficiency of smart agriculture. More agricultural output means more money in the economy, and this data-driven strategy helps farmers do just that by making better use of their resources and wasting less. Advanced solutions like sharding, sidechains etc. enable the effective handling of voluminous data related to smart agriculture and healthcare services. Layer 2 protocols, like ARCnet, Asynchronous Transfer Mode, Econet, Ethernet, Ethernet Automatic Protection Switching, Fiber Distributed Data Interface, Frame relay, High-level Data Link Control, Lightning Network, etc., enable the efficient transfer of data in a wide area network [4]. The impact of technology has been depicted in Fig. (2).

Fig. (2))
Technological contribution to economic growth.

Encouraging Openness and Traceability in the Supply Chain

Smart agriculture uses blockchain technology to make the supply chain more transparent and traceable. This improves the quality of agricultural goods while also decreasing the possibility of fraud. Businesses may boost their income and provide economic advantages to stakeholders by charging premium rates for their products when their supply chain is more trustworthy.

Spreading Power and Encouraging Participation: Due to blockchain's decentralised structure, smaller players in smart agriculture are given more influence. Because of this openness, formerly marginalised places might see economic growth as smaller farmers are able to take part in a global marketplace.

Healthcare Decision-Making Based on Data

Decisions in healthcare may now be based on data thanks to the integration of blockchain and the Internet of Things [8]. As a result, healthcare outcomes, including diagnostic accuracy and personalized treatment strategies, are improved. The economic effect rests on the possible reduction in healthcare costs through preventative measures and early treatments, leading to a more sustainable healthcare system.

Reduced Expenses and Enhanced Efficiency

Internet of Things (IoT) devices and blockchain enable smart agriculture and healthcare to automate and streamline procedures, resulting in substantial cost savings. By streamlining processes, cutting down on red tape, and increasing operational efficiency, we can cut out the middlemen and put more money into the economy.

Simplified Obtaining Funds

When it comes to smart agriculture, blockchain technology allows for safe and transparent financial transactions. Farmers' creditworthiness is boosted by this, in addition to real-time data on crop success. The agricultural industry contributes significantly to economic growth because better investment in technology and infrastructure is made possible by improved access to finance [9].

Working Together and Interoperability

Collaboration in Industry 5.0 is enhanced by the interoperability of blockchain and IoT technologies. What this implies for smart agriculture is that all the various systems and gadgets can communicate with one another without a hitch. It makes it possible for different parties in the healthcare industry to safely share patient data with one another. Because of the pooling of resources and expertise, new ideas are born, and the economy expands as a result of such partnerships. Global trade in agriculture and healthcare is facilitated by blockchain's trust and openness in the supply chain. With self-assurance, healthcare providers and farmers may join global marketplaces, increasing their impact and boosting economies everywhere [10]. Smart contracts that promote sustainable practices in healthcare and agriculture can be integrated into blockchain-enabled systems. Incentives or premium rates should be offered to farmers and healthcare professionals who embrace eco-friendly and ethical methods, encouraging both economic development and sustainability. Data analytics, cybersecurity, and blockchain developers are in high demand as more and more Internet of Things (IoT) applications use blockchain technology. As a result, the changing face of Industry 5.0 helps to foster economic growth by creating jobs and improving people's abilities. In the age of Industry 5.0, the synergies between blockchain and Internet of Things applications in smart agriculture and healthcare greatly contribute to economic growth by improving transparency, efficiency, and cooperation. They also spur innovation and inclusive development [11].

Social Impact on Farmers and Healthcare Sector

Smart agriculture and healthcare that uses blockchain and the IoT have far-reaching societal effects, especially on farmers and the healthcare industry as a whole. Fig. (3) depicts the impact of smart agriculture on society. There are far-reaching consequences on people's and communities' well-being brought about by these technologies, which boost efficiency and production. By giving them access to trustworthy digital platforms, blockchain-enabled smart agriculture gives smallholder farmers more agency. Because of this inclusion, they are able to have a bigger say in the agricultural ecosystem, participate more effectively in marketplaces, and get fair pay for their goods. Smart agriculture, which makes use of blockchain technology and the Internet of Things, helps farmers maximise their harvests by reducing waste [12]. Consequently, this results in higher incomes and better living conditions. Improved agricultural efficiency would allow farmers to put more money into their communities and provide a better life for their families.

Better access to financial services for farmers is made possible by blockchain's transparent and traceable nature. It becomes much simpler to acquire loans and other financial instruments for investments in agriculture when they can prove their trustworthiness using smart contracts and real-time data on crop conditions. Blockchain and the Internet of Things bring farmers together, strengthening their bonds as a community. As a result of farmers sharing information on things like weather, pests, and crop performance, agricultural communities are better equipped to work together to solve problems and learn from one another [13]. Blockchain and the Internet of Things (IoT) can make healthcare more accessible. By facilitating communication between healthcare practitioners and patients in underserved or far-flung locations, technologies like remote monitoring and telemedicine help those who might otherwise have trouble getting the treatment they need when they need it. The use of blockchain technology in healthcare guarantees the privacy, integrity, and ownership of patient records. By putting the patient first, this method encourages open communication and cooperation between doctors and their patients by giving them greater agency over their health records. By integrating blockchain technology with IoT devices in healthcare, health metrics may be continuously monitored. This makes it easier to implement preventative healthcare measures, as problems may be detected sooner, which improves health in general and lowers healthcare expenses [14]. Protecting the confidentiality of medical and agricultural records is what blockchain technology is all about. People can trust that their medical information will be kept secure, and farmers may communicate data about agricultural conditions without worrying about it being manipulated. As a result, a feeling of safety and reliability is fostered in these areas.

Fig. (3))
Impact of smart agriculture and healthcare on society.

The social benefit of smart agriculture also includes environmental care, which brings us to our ninth point. Farmers can make the world a better place by improving environmental conditions with the use of Internet of Things (IoT) devices to optimise resource utilisation and blockchain technology to ensure sustainable practices. Education and skill development are essential for the adoption of blockchain and the Internet of Things. Knowledge of how to use technology, understand data, and manage systems is useful for both farmers and medical professionals [15]. In addition to enhancing their own abilities, this helps communities economically and socially as a whole. The Internet of Things (IoT) and blockchain technology have far-reaching societal implications for smart agriculture and healthcare. These technologies are crucial in bringing about beneficial social change in several areas, such as healthcare (by making it more accessible and increasing patient outcomes) and agriculture (by giving farmers more control over their livelihoods).

The advancement of blockchain identity management will mitigate the risk of identity theft. The technologies like self-sovereign identity framework, decentralized identifiers, verifiable credentials, etc., empower individuals to attain complete control over their personal data in the field of agriculture and the healthcare system.

Role of the Industrial Sector in Sustainable Development

When it comes to smart agriculture and healthcare, the industrial sector is crucial for making the most of blockchain-enabled Internet of Things applications, particularly within the context of Industry 5.0. Industrial actors may take advantage of this revolutionary convergence of technologies to boost efficiency, promote innovation, and contribute to sustainable development. Industrial firms are leading the charge for smart agricultural innovation through blockchain and IoT integration. They put money into research and development of cutting-edge technology, including sensor networks, automated machinery, and blockchain-enabled systems, to improve farming operations [16]. The industrial sector can optimise the whole agricultural supply chain by leveraging blockchain technology and its huge supply chain networks. Agricultural product production, processing, and distribution may all be monitored and verified in this way. Distributed ledger technology (blockchain) improves supply chain accountability and quality control by making transactions transparent and traceable. Fig. (4) denotes contribution of Industrial sector in sustainable economic growth and development.

Industry 5.0 places an emphasis on the interdependence of different systems. With the help of industrial players, smart agricultural and healthcare ecosystems may integrate varied components, allowing for smooth connections between blockchain networks and IoT devices. The overall performance and efficiency of the system are improved by these interconnections. Data management and analysis are areas where the industrial sector thrives. Industrial actors can build strong analytics platforms using the vast quantities of data produced by IoT devices in healthcare and agriculture. By guaranteeing the data's confidentiality and integrity, blockchain lays a solid groundwork for well-informed decisions. Industrial players may help with the process of standardising protocols and interfaces in applications that use blockchain technology to enable the Internet of Things [17]. With these standards in place, smart agriculture and healthcare may more easily integrate various devices and systems, creating an ecosystem that is more coherent and interoperable. Improving efficiency is the core focus of smart manufacturing processes in Industry 5.0. By incorporating blockchain and IoT technology into production processes, the industrial sector can guarantee that production data is transparent and intact. This, in turn, helps in the manufacturing of dependable and trackable farm and medical apparatus.

Fig. (4))
Contribution of the industrial sector to sustainable economic growth and development.

Industrial players have the financial means to pour a lot of money into R&D projects pertaining to blockchain and Internet of Things (IoT) applications. For smart agriculture and healthcare, this means creating specialised sensors, communication protocols, and blockchain solutions. Healthcare and agriculture data are particularly sensitive; thus, it is crucial that the industrial sector takes the lead in establishing strong cybersecurity safeguards. To ensure the privacy and security of data in ecosystems enabled by blockchain technology, it is necessary to design secure communication routes, encryption mechanisms, and access control systems. Companies in the industrial sector may encourage partnerships with blockchain and Internet of Things (IoT) innovators and startups [18]. Larger industrial corporations may benefit from new ideas and technology for smart agriculture and healthcare by teaming up with smaller, more nimble businesses. Smart agriculture and healthcare are two areas where the industrial sector may promote and execute sustainable practices. The agriculture sector can do its part to improve environmental and social conditions by adopting more sustainable production practices and using blockchain technology to track the origin of goods. The success of Industry 5.0 hinges on the industrial sector's ability to utilise blockchain-enabled Internet of Things applications for smart agriculture and healthcare. The successful and long-term incorporation of these technologies into contemporary healthcare and agriculture depends on industry actors driving positive transitions through standardisation, collaboration, and innovation [8].

Stakeholders and NGOs’ Interest

In order to successfully deploy and manage smart agriculture, which integrates blockchain and the Internet of Things (IoT), stakeholders and non-governmental organisations (NGOs) are vital. Their participation is critical for the agriculture industry to tackle many issues, promote inclusivity, and implement sustainable methods. Fig. (5) shows the roles of stakeholder in promotion Blockchain and IoT usage in agriculture and health care ecosystem. By bringing attention to the issue among farmers, legislators, and community members, NGOs and other interested parties may push for the integration of blockchain and IoT into smart agriculture. Their work in spreading the word about these technologies' advantages, building awareness, and calming fears is crucial. Non-governmental organisations (NGOs) may help farmers learn how to use blockchain and the Internet of Things (IoT) by organising training programmes and capacity-building projects. Part of this is training people to set up and operate smart devices so farmers can make data-driven decisions [19]. In order to help farmers, particularly smallholders, access capital and resources for smart agricultural solutions, stakeholders and NGOs may play a key role. To make sure that the financial hurdles to adopting new technologies are minimised, this support might take the form of grants, subsidies, or collaborations with financial institutions. It is crucial to involve local communities and farmers in the decision-making processes when it comes to smart agriculture implementation. By mediating between different parties, NGOs may make sure that everyone's opinions are taken into consideration and that technological solutions are tailored to meet the requirements of the community. When it comes to integrating blockchain and the Internet of Things (IoT) in agriculture, non-governmental organisations (NGOs) may help set standards and best practices. The constant use of technology across diverse areas and agricultural methods is ensured by this framework for uniformity and interoperability [20].

Sustainable agriculture techniques may be advanced by stakeholders and non-governmental organisations. Some examples of this include pushing for more transparent and environmentally friendly supply chains made possible by blockchain technology and promoting the use of precision farming methods made possible by Internet of Things devices to reduce resource use. When it comes to promoting policies that help, NGOs are crucial on a global, national, and even local scale. Together with governments, they can solve data privacy and security issues and promote the proper use of blockchain and the Internet of Things (IoT) in agriculture [4]. To ensure the success of smart agricultural initiatives, ongoing monitoring and assessment are crucial. In order to make these programmes more effective overall, stakeholders and NGOs may help by performing impact evaluations, finding ways to improve them, and sharing what they have learned. When it comes to smart agriculture, NGOs may fight for farmers' rights to data ownership and privacy protection. They can try to put systems in place that give farmers more say over their data and make them more transparent about its use in the blockchain ecosystem. It is essential for NGOs, stakeholders, and other players in the agricultural value chain to collaborate with one another. Together, these groups can better manage blockchain and Internet of Things (IoT) solutions for smart agriculture by combining their resources and knowledge.
