Furthermore, during the first phases, individuals within the health ecosystem readily embrace the innovations. In the future, Blockchain’s comprehensive perspective will aim to resolve issues that now impact the healthcare industry. It enables doctors, patients, and chemists to get all the accessible information at a particular time. Medical companies are actively researching and testing blockchain technology to manage health information in the medical profession. The healthcare industry has established itself as an essential tool by adhering to pharmaceutical practices, enhancing payment alternatives, and dispersing patient health information. Blockchain, machine learning, and AI are crucial to the medical industry. Blockchain’s medical transformation has valid uses. The program utilizes blockchain monitoring technologies to optimize the medical supply chain [17]. “Blockchain is an advanced data storage system that securely stores an individual’s health history, including diagnoses, test results, previous treatments, and even readings from intelligent sensors. This strategy allows a clinician to get all the relevant information efficiently to make accurate diagnoses and recommendations. Due to the comprehensive storage of data inside a singular Blockchain system, it is impervious to loss and alteration.” An entity may use blockchain to bypass its internal networks. An intricate network of several entities, each with different degrees of authority, operating on a secure blockchain database has the potential to protect businesses from external threats and attacks. Implementing a blockchain network in a healthcare organization may minimize rescue assaults and other issues like computer corruption or hardware failure [18].

HEALTHCARE BENEFITS OF BLOCKCHAIN-BASED TECHNOLOGY

Blockchain technology has several advantages in healthcare, tackling diverse obstacles and enhancing medical systems’ overall effectiveness, safety, and openness. Fig. (2) highlights blockchain’s medical advantages.

Several notable advantages involve the following:

Ease of Access

Individuals using applications equipped with blockchain technology may securely, conveniently, and efficiently retrieve their medical data.

The Ability to Interoperate

Traditional approaches rely on a centralized data storage system, which adds to the difficulties in achieving seamless compatibility across patients’ records. These problems may be avoided due to the need for a centralized database in blockchain technology. Moreover, all nodes can interact with each other.

Proof of Identity

The blockchain’s block-based authentication of data scheme uses a distinct private key combined with a publicly accessible key.

Fig. (2))
Benefits of blockchain in the healthcare sector.

Decentralzsed Repository

The decentralized structure of blockchain makes it well-suited for storing vast quantities of data across different places. Decentralized storage enables enhanced data quality, expedited medical data retrieval, and elevated security levels.

Cost Saving

Implementing blockchain technology in the healthcare sector might substantially decrease operating expenses by optimizing administrative processes, improving compatibility across different systems, and facilitating expedited data transmission. Using blockchain technology, healthcare providers may streamline several labor-intensive and paper-dependent procedures in the healthcare industry, including exchanging medical records, managing medication supply chains, and processing insurance claims. This leads to significant reductions in expenses. Moreover, blockchain technology has the potential to provide real-time and precise information on the transportation and distribution of drugs and medical supplies. Improved transparency in the supply chain system reduces the probability of counterfeit products penetrating, enhancing effectiveness and reducing losses caused by counterfeit items.

NETWORKS OF BLOCKCHAIN TECHNOLOGY

Blockchain may be categorized into several groups, each with its unique traits, and their activities reflect the overall behavior of the network. Alhadhrami et al. (2017) argued that these blockchains may be divided into three major categories [19].

Public Blockchain

A public blockchain is distinguished by its inclusiveness, enabling unfettered participation and active involvement from any person. This indicates that people from diverse backgrounds can engage as users or miners and that anybody may contribute new blocks. This ensures the existence of transparency in public blockchain networks. A public blockchain, also known as a permissionless blockchain, allows anybody to get a copy of the blockchain and participate in the validation of blocks. Bitcoin and Ethereum exemplify the concept of a public blockchain.

Private Blockchain

A private blockchain is a limited blockchain that is suitable for specific organizations. An organization establishes the criteria for determining who can access and maintain a shared ledger. Hyperledger is an example of a private blockchain.

Consortium Blockchain

In blockchain technology, a consortium blockchain is a kind of blockchain maintained by many organizations that needs permission to access. By combining private blockchain technology’s benefits with the transparency typical of a public blockchain, the consortium blockchain offers a unique combination of both. A hybrid blockchain is another name for a consortium blockchain. Dragonchain is an example of a consortium blockchain.

THE METHOD FOR VERIFYING BLOCKCHAIN

The blockchain validation mechanism, commonly called mining, is recognized by specific algorithms, such as proof of work. This function is executed using a consensus technique, establishing the rules that nodes must adhere to verify the blocks. The consensus process ensures that all validating nodes will obtain a favorable outcome by following the correct sequence of transactions. The decision to include the block in the chain is at their discretion [20]. Consensus mechanisms are specially designed to authenticate blocks on a blockchain, enhancing the

network’s security. Below is a compilation of the vital healthcare protocols about blockchain that have been released so far.

Understanding Practical Byzantine Fault Tolerance PBFT in Blockchain

The network’s nodes may be categorized into two distinct groups: clients and servers. The client node establishes a connection with a server node by sending a request. The server node then disseminates the transaction to the other server nodes. Once a server node accepts the transaction, it tells the other server nodes of its readiness to execute the transaction. The PBFT uses this technique to authenticate the transactions. Castro & Liskov (1999) stated that for an interaction to be deemed verified, it must satisfy three conditions [21]:

1. It must be confirmed by many nodes, which is also known as a “Confirmation Alert”,

2. Each validated node must broadcast a message to the network confirming its action,

3. The sender node must get a response.

Proof-of-Stake (PoS) in Blockchain

Proof-of-stake decreases the computational workload needed to verify blocks and transactions. Through proof-of-work, the integrity and safety of the blockchain were effectively maintained. Proof-of-stake modifies the block verification process by using the machines possessed by currency holders, hence reducing the computational effort needed. Owners provide their currencies as collateral to verify blocks and take on the role of validators. Validators are selected at random to authenticate transactions and validate block data. This method employs a non-competitive reward-based approach, such as proof-of-work, to determine who will earn fees. A coin owner must “stake” certain coins to become a validator. For example, a user must stake a minimum of 32 ETH to become a validator on the Ethereum network. Blocks undergo validation by many validators, and upon receiving verification from a certain number of validators, the block is deemed accurate, finalized, and closed. Various proof procedures use different approaches to attain agreement. Nodes in the network participate in the process of verifying new blocks and earning fees by staking a certain amount of bitcoin. The method then chooses a node from the candidate pool to authenticate the new block. This selection mechanism uses the deposit quantity of cryptocurrencies in conjunction with other parameters, such as coin age and a randomization procedure, to ensure fairness for all participants in the network [22].

Understanding Proof-of-work (PoW) in Blockchain Technology

“Proof of work is a consensus process determining which network users, known as miners, are authorized to undertake the profitable duty of validating new data. The mining process is profitable because the miners are incentivized with newly generated cryptocurrency to properly verify fresh data without engaging in fraudulent activities. Proof of work is a software algorithm employed by Bitcoin and other blockchains to verify the validity of blocks based on the condition that they necessitate a specific level of computational power for their creation,” explains Amaury Sechet, the creator of the cryptocurrency e-Cash. “It is a consensus mechanism that enables anonymous entities in decentralized networks to have trust in each other.” The term “work” in the concept of evidence of work is crucial. The system mandates that miners engage in a competitive race to answer complex mathematical riddles to deter any attempts to manipulate the system. The victor of this race is chosen to append the most recent data or transactions to the blockchain. The reward for winning miners is contingent upon verifying the accuracy and validity of the data added to the chain by other participants in the network [23].

Understanding Proof-of-Weight in Blockchain

Proof-of-weight consensus incorporates the concept of “weight” into the fundamental idea of PoW, as determined by algorithm consensus. The weights are directly proportional to the values produced by the nodes in the network. The objective is to prevent “double-spending,” in which a customer might use the same digital token twice by manipulating its relative weight [24].

Proof-of-Existence (POE) in Blockchain

This method is designed to verify the existence of documents at a particular time. Data ownership information could be disclosed without revealing the data. The POE paradigm provides evidence of intellectual property papers, such as patents [25].

DATA REGARDING BLOCKCHAIN-BASED MONITORING

The issue of fraudulent or counterfeit drugs is growing fast within the healthcare industry. The use of such counterfeit pharmaceuticals could endanger the patient’s well-being. The proliferation of these counterfeit pharmaceuticals [26] will have adverse consequences for the pharmaceutical business responsible for their production and any other entities associated with their distribution. Hence, it is essential to develop a robust pharmaceutical tracking system to combat counterfeit drugs. Nations throughout the globe are undertaking substantial endeavors to enhance drug monitoring systems. By implementing a secure drug traceability system, individuals such as patients and other relevant parties may conveniently monitor the whereabouts of medicine along the drug supply chain and verify its authenticity [27]. The present medication monitoring systems lack essential components for the pharmaceutical supply chain. The existing healthcare system’s lack of transparency may potentially contribute to issues such as medicine shortages, opioid addiction, and the presence of counterfeit drugs. Due to the lack of transparency in the drug supply chain, it is difficult for patients and other stakeholders to monitor the whereabouts of their medications at any given moment. This issue is significant since the well-being of patients might be contingent upon the effectiveness of the treatments. By utilizing the resources and measures outlined in this treaty and protocol, we can effectively safeguard public health, halt the proliferation of counterfeit medication, and emerge victorious in our battle against them at national and local levels. We aim to establish a unified and comprehensive strategy to eradicate counterfeit tablets while promoting uniformity regarding definitions, information resources, and tools. This concerted effort aims to eliminate the problem.

FACILITATORS OF BLOCKCHAIN TECHNOLOGY FOR REVITALIZING MEDICAL FACILITIES

Several industries and medical-care supporters or providers have been involved in research and investigations into the use of blockchain techniques in healthcare and its key areas. According to Le Nguyen (2018), “these observed providers, including BurstIQ, Guardtime, Robomed, Simply Vital, Encrypgen, Chronicled, and Tieion, are among the select agencies that provide and promote Blockchain technology at grassroots levels” [28]. Blockchain entails the creation of novel patient data cards for medical professionals in different healthcare facilities. Adding new knowledge often involves unnecessary repetition, resulting in a waste of time and a significant disruption to one’s health. Each person’s credentials or access options may vary based on their position within the supply chain.

Furthermore, each block carrying the pharmaceutical information would be linked to another block by a hash. Moreover, the data transparency functionality in the Blockchain framework would facilitate the identification of the whole origin and eliminate the dissemination of counterfeit medications. Javaid et al. (2020) argued, “When patients visit a new clinic, a new medical card is created for them and stored in a designated facility. Typically, these facts are insufficient for the public and consist of compiled records from the carers at the relevant institutions. Blockchain can efficiently aggregate issues in data processing. This technology aims to provide globally accessible and standardized Blockchain medical records” [29]. In healthcare, clinical studies are being carried out to evaluate the efficacy of these medicines in treating or partially alleviating certain diseases. “Scientists can document data about test results, individual identifiers, medical records, and several other characteristics. It is essential to verify the authenticity of data gathered during clinical trials to ensure that scientists, pharmaceutical companies, and policymakers may have complete confidence in the reliability of the outcomes. The utilization of blockchain technology can augment transparency and responsibility in the realm of clinical trials. The use of blockchain technology in the healthcare industry provides significant advantages in terms of recordkeeping efficiency. The blocks containing medical data can be accessed by professionals and patients, ensuring that the processing of medical history considers patient concerns and needs. Blockchain technology in the supply chain is widely adopted and particularly suitable for the healthcare sector, specifically for medications” [30].

Blockchain facilitates efficient and proactive monitoring of the procedures and services offered by healthcare providers. Within the healthcare sector, blockchain technology streamlines and expedites the approval procedure for acquisition and processing. Employing Blockchain technology and minimizing waste by queuing in an organized manner is advantageous to enhance efficiency and streamline workflow. It has emerged as one of the most widely used and sought-after technologies in the present day. Omar et al. (2021) argued that “this will transform the healthcare business into a dependable and secure digital directory. Blockchain healthcare solutions enhance several issues, including clinical trials, maintenance of patient information, and traceability of prescriptions” [31]. Blockchains may effectively be used in the healthcare sector to facilitate informed decision-making within the health network. The decentralized blockchain platform gives the healthcare industry prospects to detect and prevent fraudulent activities, decrease operational expenses, efficiently create employment possibilities, remove redundant work, and provide transparency in the healthcare ecosystem.

Moreover, it is used to maintain assets, such as permanence, trust, and decentralization. Patient confidentiality is enhanced, increasing confidence in protecting their medical records. Additionally, there is an increased likelihood of receiving accurate diagnoses, enhancing the prospects for further medical treatment [32]. Patients will now have an enhanced ability to express their preferences and concerns while processing their health data. Partners of the Blockchain network will have permission to trade data, which will enhance privacy and control. The commitment to blockchain technology has far-reaching implications for all parties involved in the healthcare industry. Diverse networks are anticipated to collaborate to provide insights and assess the significance of therapy based on this technology. Implementing a nationwide blockchain network for electronic medical records will enhance operational efficiency and provide enhanced patient health outcomes in the future. “Blockchain is a decentralized and immutable ledger that records transactions between linked blocks and is stored in a digital register” [33]. Healthcare data such as patient records, logs of medical equipment, and temperature monitoring of medical items may be documented throughout the distribution process by the requirements of the medical industry.

INNOVATIONS IN HEALTHCARE UTILIZING BLOCKCHAIN TECHNOLOGY: A UNIFIED WORKFLOW PROCESS

Abdellatif et al. (2020) stated that blockchain solutions in healthcare sectors provide several interconnected benefits, including efficient administration of master patient indices, streamlined claim adjustments, secure medical supply chain management, enhanced interoperability, and accurate individual and longitudinal data recording [34]. “The interactive work-process flow begins with the distributed network flow, digitalized transactions, and shared data and ledger. These elements allow Blockchain drivers to focus more on improving healthcare services, making them healthier and more inventive than ever” [35]. The fundamental concept of Blockchain technology is simple and continuously developing, expanding the network of blocks to accommodate the diverse requirements and distinct attributes of many businesses. The autonomous Blockchain architecture offers a comprehensive monitoring solution and enables real-time updating of findings. Blockchain technology will significantly reduce financial failures and prevent theft and the unauthorized movement of information. It can address issues related to dynamic outcomes and unauthorized access to data. It enables the transmission of enduring, time-stamped clinical study data and outcomes, reducing fraud and errors in clinical studies. The health business has predominantly embraced Blockchain technology [36].

Blockchain is a pervasive technology that impacts every sector to some extent. Blockchain technology is used, especially in regions where it is necessary to establish trust among many parties and stakeholders. “Blockchain has the potential to significantly change the process of obtaining informed permission, which patients now experience as fragmented when signing consent forms for appointments, clinical procedures, or medical tests. Blockchain’s promises for these applications include facilitating the interchange of clinical test data and the potential for uncovering advantages for testing participants. Blockchain has the potential to become a crucial component in managing healthcare consents, enabling efficient sharing of information. Patients can connect with other institutions through Blockchain technology [37, 38] and get their medical information seamlessly.”

Jayaraman et al. (2021) argued, “A validated blockchain is a restricted network with links to the networks of all participants in the system. Therefore, it is created and used inside organizations and enterprises to exchange information and facilitate secure transactions” [39]. After achieving consensus, a transaction is deemed a permanent entry and appended to the existing blockchain as a novel block. “The internet promotes and motivates individuals to construct their websites. Every user within the blockchain network may communicate with others within the same network without requiring authorization by establishing their unique network address” [40]. “A supply chain system powered by blockchain ensures the security, authenticity, and punctuality of the delivery of pharmaceutical supplies. It enables the maker to adhere to the precise formulation mix per medical specifications. Medical devices can collect patient data, authenticate the therapy with the intended patient, and anonymously share procedural data with patients and regulators.” The healthcare industry finds blockchain technology captivating. Medical science has made significant advancements in recent decades, offering high-quality medical treatments. It is a widely used, decentralized, and transparent digital system employed across several computers to oversee and track transactions. It is acknowledged for its substantial impact on many markets and sectors. The presence of this technology tackles problems that current methods are unable to handle. Superlative healthcare necessitates assurance, safeguarding, confidentiality, and seamless data exchange across disparate systems. Blockchain presents an opportunity to address the novelty of healthcare challenges [41].

Blockchain’s perpetual, independent, and fully transparent nature positions it as a potential solution for safeguarding health data. Blockchain technology allows individuals’ identities and medical information to be securely stored while ensuring system stability. This innovative technology will streamline the intricate billing process by eliminating inefficient and redundant devices. Furthermore, it may assist patients in uploading and authorizing relevant parties to access their medical records. “Blockchain technology has the potential to revolutionize the exchange of health information by enhancing the productivity, reliability, and safety of electronic medical records. Blockchain is a decentralized ledger that stores important and dispersed transaction information” [42].

BLOCKCHAIN TECHNOLOGICAL ISSUES AND SOLUTIONS

The key attributes of blockchain technology include security, guarantee, unchangeability, verification, distributed storage, data exchange, and data retrieval. The healthcare industry’s needs align with the characteristics of blockchain technology. The potential of blockchain technology in the healthcare business is vast. Various startups and established organizations are putting these prospective applications into practice. Here, we will review several uses of blockchain technology in healthcare to better understand them.

E-Health Record System

The health record system may be seen via three distinct periods. The three types of health records are paper-based, electronic, and electronic health records that use blockchain technology. The paper-based health record system exhibited inefficiency, disorganization, and insecurity, lacking tamper-proof capabilities. Electronic health records are a hybrid of traditional paper-based and digital electronic medical records. The electronic health record system is more efficient and cost effective than traditional paper-based medical records. The key functionalities of electronic health information include the digital storage of medical information, efficient administration of patient appointments, streamlined billing and accounting processes, and facilitation of laboratory testing. However, EHRs faced interoperability concerns, gaps in information, and knowledge breaches. Interoperability is the smooth transfer of data across sources. Decentralized management and lack of medical data standards lead to more patients. Physicians and hospitals can access medical data and records, but patients need aid. This leads to a situation where there is a lack of equal access to information. In addition, there have been countless instances of cyber-attacks explicitly targeting medical records. Using blockchain technology may resolve the issues of interoperability, information asymmetry, and data breach.

An elementary electronic health record system that utilizes blockchain technology operates as follows:

1. Extensive medical data is gathered from patients via mobile apps and electronic health record systems.

2. Data is encrypted and kept on the blockchain network.

3. The health advice is encrypted and then sent to individuals individually.

MeDRec, MeDShare, and other similar examples are prototypes of electronic health record systems that use blockchain technology. The Estonian medical record system, which utilizes blockchain technology, is noteworthy. The blockchain-powered electronic health record system enables Estonian individuals, healthcare providers, and health insurance companies to access comprehensive information on all medical procedures conducted in Estonia via this technology [43].

System for Detecting Medical Fraud

Ensuring drug safety is crucial since it has a direct impact on the well-being of the general population. Supply chain management is a crucial responsibility in the medical industry. Otherwise, it would be challenging to regulate the manufacturing of counterfeit pharmaceuticals, falsify packaging records, and alter labels for contaminated drugs in their distribution. The extended healthcare supply chain has several individuals and components in motion, making it susceptible to fraudulent assaults [44]. Blockchain technology enables the implementation of a sophisticated platform for monitoring and tracking.

Clinical Research

Doubts about trustworthiness arise in medical research due to the potential for data manipulation. Clinical trials are necessary to evaluate novel medications’ efficacy and potential adverse reactions. Clinical trials consist of four distinct stages. The following are included: assessment of the safety of a new medication, evaluation of both safety and effectiveness in humans, investigation of the efficacy of drug dosage and patient safety, and analysis of the long-term effectiveness of the new medicine. Approximately 80% of medical studies are non-reproducible due to various issues, including fraud, data mismanagement, and trial misconduct. Blockchain technology can guarantee transparency at different phases of clinical studies. Blockchain technology offers a decentralized mechanism for recording data, enhancing confidence among many participating in clinical studies. In addition, it guarantees openness in the communication between patients and clinicians [45].

SOFTWARE SOLUTION WITH APPLICATION OF BLOCKCHAIN IN HEALTHCARE

This section will discuss the possible uses of blockchain technology by examining current solutions in the healthcare business, namely in electronic health records and patient health records.

Gem Health Network

If an individual becomes ill while traveling to another state or abroad, they cannot contact their regular physician to get their medical records. Gem Health Network has developed a promising solution for such situations. “Gem Health Network utilizes the Ethereum blockchain technology to provide a collaborative network architecture. The Gem Health Network enables diverse healthcare practitioners to access identical healthcare information, eliminating the constraints of centralized storage. The medical records and information are pertinent and precise, and authorized users have immediate access to the most up-to-date treatment information” [46]. It encrypts the data with sophisticated cryptographic methods, keeping the sensitive information safe. Throughout the system, Byzantine Fault Tolerance (BFT) is the consensus technique used, providing well-studied fault tolerance mechanisms and the ability to continue to operate even when some nodes crash or behave maliciously. Casa holds itself to the highest standards of digital security, thanks to a group of fourteen CISOs and VPs of Security, who guide the utility of the network built by this members-own-everything infrastructure to a cooperative environment such that owners can share with other owners only what owners need to share so that owners can work better.

OmniPHR

OmniPHR is a system developed by Roehrs, Costa, and Righi to facilitate the management of patient health records. Therefore, offering patients a unified view of their health information may result in significant savings across many healthcare systems. Furthermore, the OmniPHR system aims to address the problem of healthcare providers accessing the most recent patient information, even if the data are stored elsewhere or updated by another healthcare provider. The primary concern that OmniPHR aims to address is the differentiation between Electronic Health Records and Personal Health Records. EHR, or Electronic Health Records, are documents that adhere to various statutory standards to address the challenge of consistent record-keeping across state and national boundaries [47]. This allows the records to remain up-to-date and as accurate as possible. The primary concept for data is for it to be maintained and updated by the medical professionals themselves without any input from the patients. This differs from a personal health record, where the patients manage their information. OmniPHR develops a system that effectively resolves this problem by allowing patients to access a comprehensive and accurate overview of their medical information, meeting the standards required by the healthcare community [48]. OmniPHR adopts a hybrid consensus mechanism, which combines Proof of Stake (PoS) and Practical Byzantine Fault Tolerance (PBFT) to achieve the trade-off between energy-saving and secure. This ability lets patients have control over who has access to their medical records and ensures that data exchange will be visible and traceable. Using blockchain technology, OmniPHR further enhances patient autonomy, data integrity, and trust in health computer systems.

MedRec

The Metric system manages decentralized electronic medical records (EMRs). “It was developed by Azaria, Ekblaw, Vieira, and Lippman using blockchain technology.” The platform allows patients consistent access to health data across many healthcare providers and treatment facilities [49]. “MedRec enables medical partners to participate as miners by giving them access to comprehensive, de-identified data as mining rewards, which are obtained from their support and verification of the system via Proof of Work (PoW). MedRec is a functional prototype that primarily focuses on analyzing the approach and implementation of the system before doing any field testing” [50]. However, the MedRec system was only deployed on a small scale inside the same hospital foundation in Israel. This system uses a blockchain system based on Ethereum [51]. MeDRec uses cryptographic and asymmetric algorithms that make data encryption very robust for protecting patient records. Proof-of-authority is a consensus technique through which transactions can be authenticated only by authenticated nodes, ensuring security and efficiency at optimal levels. Moreover, this technology helps the patients to have control over their data and allows them to provide access to the medical professionals selected by them. It also maintains a clear record of all instances of data access.

MedShare

MedShare’s objective is to enable secure data interchange across different stakeholders in the healthcare sector. MeDShare utilizes blockchain technology and cloud services to provide the highest security and privacy for medical data. MeDShare employs a sophisticated smart contract and access control mechanism to oversee the data and revoke access from malicious trade [52].

IRYO

This platform is designed for sharing and keeping EHS (Environment, Health, and Safety) data. It ensures the protection of the data storage system by retaining at least one encrypted live copy of the data, thereby safeguarding it from possible attacks. Using this approach makes it feasible to guarantee the protection of all types of health information. “Before using AI-related data, the research institution must undergo IRYO validation. Researchers must use the IRYO research software tool to initiate a new inquiry” [53]. If a researcher’s study question meets the criteria of a patient, a notification will be sent to the patient’s phone. Upon obtaining the patient’s agreement, the data is promptly sent to the research organization, and the patient is reimbursed with tokens as a reward for their involvement. Upon the completion of the study, the patient will receive a notification on his mobile device providing the investigation results. Researchers can examine either fully anonymous or partly anonymized datasets inside the system. The IRYO network provides a means to access a public blockchain. Each storage node in the EOS blockchain is required to provide patients with cryptographic evidence by recording hashes. The CHT token, developed by Coral Health, may be used on their Ethereum-powered network to exchange electronic health information and genetic test outcomes in personalized medicine [54]. Implementing a personalized medicine strategy for treatment can decrease undesirable side effects while enhancing effectiveness.

Patientory

The Patientory (PTOY) platform uses the Ethereum blockchain to tokenize and share electronic health record (EHR) data. Patients in the conventional system can get duplicates of their medical information to share with doctors. Meanwhile, physicians may use mobile healthcare applications to monitor their patients’ status while on the go. However, under the existing conventional framework, these procedures are time-consuming, and doctors need more access to information [55]. Moreover, classical systems are not completely secure because of the centrality problem. The proposed system facilitates users’ access to and administration of their medical information and the records of their family members and other healthcare providers. The primary emphasis throughout system development was on complying with the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act regulation.

COMPLIANCE WITH REGULATIONS WHEN HANDLING AND SHARING DATA

Technical agreements on these platforms are expected to maintain standards without the need for human oversight. They secure data storage and transmission by using encryption techniques like curvature cryptography (Elliptic Curve Cryptography). This is a dynamically managed permission system that gives and takes away access to information as required. The immutable technology gives an extensive audit trail that improves trust. It keeps track of the way data interacts, including timestamps and details of the users, to ensure compliance with laws like HIPAA and GDPR.

Automated Consent Management

Smart agreements can grant patients immediate rights to data sharing and require that their latest permissions and the current regulations concerning data-sharing are maintained. These contracts make sure that data is gathered and processed only for the purpose and gets auto-deleted or anonymized once it is no longer required, abiding by the GDPR. Automated breach reporting can also be carried out by smart contracts, which notify regulators, along with anyone impacted, and ensure data privacy. This trust is re-enforced by a fully automated regulatory compliance that covers most of the relevant regulations in data management,

including the control of patient data, the security measures of its frame, and its storage.

Implementation of Global Data Standards

It is essential to encourage the use of global data standards, such as Level 7 Healthcare Rapid Interoperability Resources (HL7 FHIR), to improve compatibility between blockchain-based systems and conventional electronic health record systems. HL7 FHIR offers a structured system for sharing medical data, promoting seamless integration and compatibility of various medical data sources. It is important to adopt international data standards like HL7 FHIR for effectively assimilating blockchain-based systems to traditional EHR systems. HL7 FHIR has a uniform structure for data exchange that ensures accurate and constant information transfer across all the systems. It simplifies integration while reducing costs and complexity. The use of HL7 FHIR enhances the compatibility that helps the blockchain solutions interact seamlessly with EHR systems. It enables unrestricted and secure data exchange among hospitals, laboratories, clinics, and patients, creates a unified healthcare ecosystem, and improves data sharing and coordination. High-quality and reliable data entry reduces inconsistencies and errors while improving the reliability of patient records. Standardized data formats also encourage innovation, which allows researchers to create innovative applications and services that can be integrated with existing systems.

Implementation Strategies

A comprehensive approach that involves collaboration, education, pilot projects, and incentives is required and important to promote the adoption of HL7 FHIR. It is crucial to encourage cooperation among healthcare providers, regulatory agencies, and IT suppliers as it helps establish common standards and best practices for implementation. To facilitate the implementation of HL7 FHIR standards, healthcare IT professionals must be provided with extensive training and resources that will help in bridging the knowledge gaps and familiarize them with HL7 FHIR. Also, implementing pilot projects will help in demonstrating the real-world benefits of HL7 FHIR, encouraging wider acceptance in the industry. Furthermore, to motivate healthcare organizations to prioritize interoperability efforts, incentives like technical support, financial assistance, and recognition programs can be offered. To achieve wider acceptance of HL7 FHIR, combining collaboration, real-world validation, education, and incentivization is important as it will enhance data accuracy, compatibility, and advancement in the healthcare sector.

EMERGING DEVELOPMENTS IN THE USE OF BLOCKCHAIN TECHNOLOGY IN THE HEALTHCARE

Blockchain Technology in the Supply Chains of Medical Care

Blockchain technology facilitates the digital supply chain and enhances transparency and efficiency. Medical care administrations include producing and distributing commodities across the supply chain to ensure authenticity and detect any signs of manipulation or adverse environmental conditions. When a manufacturer identifies an issue with a sensor or medicine, the blockchain facilitates the seller’s recall process by quickly identifying the specific location in the supply chain where the problem originated. For example, implementing the federal medication supply chain security is expected to enhance the issue of dirty, misplaced, and hazardous pharmaceuticals. There are limited experimental schemes under this legislation that assess the effectiveness of data security measures for sharing information in medical projects. Walmart and IBM have chosen a trial method that enhances the security of recommending and sourcing drugs [56].

Rapid Integration into the IT Revolution

The Internet technological revolution, including artificial intelligence, cloud computing, and blockchain advancements, may be seen as further innovations that have accelerated the adoption of innovative technologies. The unique hybrid cloud architecture of the client offers highly accessible public cloud services for storing sensitive medical information behind secure firewalls, allowing for accurate estimation of regular requirements [57]. Artificial intelligence utilizes pattern recognition to extract and integrate elements from a vast amount of data stored in interconnected cloud networks, which are securely organized using blockchain frameworks. By integrating linked sensors with the Internet of Things, blockchain technology may effectively reveal demographic trends, accelerate research and development in the medical field, and ultimately improve patient outcomes.

Rapid Advancements in Developed Markets

Generally, affluent nations, especially those in Asia, benefit from the development of blockchain technology. The Chinese government has strategically positioned itself to influence the blockchain industry by issuing a more significant number of blockchain copyrights. As per prominent Chinese online newspaper reports, “China’s State Council typically implements blockchain development as part of its 13th five-year plan. In 2019, President Xi of China recognized the crucial importance of blockchain technology in strengthening China’s position in cyberspace, creating a digital economy, and fostering social and economic progress.” Therefore, in 2018, the United Arab Emirates (UAE) implemented a strategic plan in Estonia to use blockchain technology to improve critical aspects of citizens’ lives, such as banking, transportation, and healthcare. The medical care stage exhibits a data-sharing outcome from proficient medical practitioners, including medical specialists and technological and local organizations, to preserve and disseminate assessment data.

Implement Decentralized Identity Systems

Patient data governance has revolutionized with decentralized identifier (DID) systems, empowering patients to have full ownership, privacy, and security over their healthcare data. In DID (Decentralized Identity) systems, patients maintain data ownership and do not need a central database by using blockchain and cryptographic solutions. Every patient has an individual digital identifier (a digital ID) stored in a decentralized network, which begins with verifying one's identity and deciding who has access. Patients have full control to grant and revoke access to their data, enabling only trusted parties (e.g., healthcare professionals, insurers, researchers, etc.) to access the information they require. It does this whilst improving privacy, mitigating the risks of centralized data breaches, allowing for data to be shared between healthcare systems, supporting a continuum of care, and data-driven decision-making. Patients get more control over their dossier, can track who has accessed their records, and have their anonymity protected in data mining about their illness. Centralized DID systems transfer control over information back to consumers, allowing them to be the masters of their data, not to mention that, in turn, this reaffirms the need for a privacy-prone, secure, and patient-centric healthcare system inherently needed in this digital era.

Utilize Advanced Encryption Techniques

Utilizing cryptographic algorithms such as homomorphic encryption and zero-knowledge proofs can ameliorate the security of patient data on existing systems with the added ability to use such workloads natively for analytics and processing. Homomorphic encryption is one where one can compute the encrypted data without revealing its decrypted form to the server, keeping anonymity throughout the computation going on. This means that institutions can independently perform statistical research or apply machine learning algorithms on the encrypted patient datasets, sharing patient-specific insights without the need to directly access the confidential raw data. This philosophy still depends on secure, deep-operators that maintain the privacy of protected data; even during complex data operations. Zero-knowledge proofs (ZKPs) allow one party to prove the truth of a statement without revealing any data besides the validity of the statement. For example, in healthcare, ZKPs can serve to safeguard personal data through patient identification, medical records verification, and transaction data verification without exposing the patient's personal information. This hides confidential information when data exchange takes place, and there is a lesser risk of unauthorized access or breaches. This combination of strategies builds a whole mechanism that allows patient data to be kept safe, and confidentiality only gets breached on a need-to-know basis. These include tools like homomorphic encryption and zero-knowledge proofs (ZKPs), which enable care providers and researchers to run the analyses they require without opening the door to entire medical records. This dual application enables the two (health and privacy) to be used simultaneously and promotes big data usage in the health industry by protecting patient's trust in healthcare.

OBSTACLES RELATED TO THE INCORPORATION OF BLOCKCHAIN TECHNOLOGY INTO HEALTHCARE SYSTEMS

Scalability Issues

Conventional blockchains like Ethereum and Bitcoin have a restricted ability to process transactions, enabling only a few transactions per second, which is insufficient for healthcare systems handling massive amounts of patient data. Additionally, on-chain storage becomes unfeasible due to the large volume of healthcare data, which includes imaging, medical records, and genomes, while off-chain storage adds complexity and potential failure. Moreover, network latency in blockchain transactions can cause significant delays in data updates and retrieval that are unacceptable in medical situations where urgent and prompt access is needed.

Integration Complexity

To achieve blockchain compatibility with healthcare standards like HL7 and FHIR, significant development and adaptation are required. Several healthcare companies depend on outdated systems that are not designed for blockchain, rendering the upgrades costly and time-consuming. Real-time synchronizations are required to ensure consistency across blockchain and conventional databases. Creating and implementing intelligent contracts for healthcare requires expertise to decrease risks like inaccurate execution and data breaches.

High Implementation Costs

Implementing a blockchain infrastructure requires substantial upfront investments in hardware, software, and personnel, which represents a significant hurdle for smaller healthcare providers. Ongoing expenses are incurred due to ongoing maintenance, including upgrades, security patches, and node management. A shortage of competent experts to build and oversee blockchain systems results in costly staff hiring or training. Complying with healthcare legislation, such as HIPAA in the US and GDPR in Europe, requires significant investment in legal and compliance expertise. Additionally, blockchains that rely on Proof-of-Work (PoW) require a substantial amount of energy, resulting in costly operations. While other consensus methods, such as Proof-of-Stake, have been investigated, they also have their own set of difficulties and restrictions.

Security and Privacy Concerns

Proper management of encryption keys is important to ensure the privacy of the data while blockchain offers encryption. Weakness in the blockchain network can lead to data breaches that will require strong security processes. Detailed control models are required to implement access control methods to restrict access to sensitive healthcare data. It is also important to protect healthcare information by restricting access and modifications to authorized individuals to ensure data integrity and security.

Consensus Mechanisms and Performance

Consensus mechanisms based on blockchain technology have a significant influence on the performance and security of healthcare systems. Proof-of-work (PoW) is a secure method, but it is more practical as it consumes a significant amount of energy and has a poor ability to process transactions. While requiring security and optimization in the healthcare sector, proof-of-stake (PoS) also provides energy efficiency and higher scalability. Advantages like faster transaction rates and energy efficiency are offered by delegated proof-of-stake (DPoS) and Byzantine fault tolerance (BFT). However, their complex implantations and restricted testing healthcare situations present several complications.

Data Governance and Ownership

Ensuring patient data sovereignty is of utmost importance and requires procedures that allow patients to control and regulate access to their health information. This ensures compliance with legal and ethical standards by addressing issues related to data ownership and use, especially in decentralized environments. It is important that a thorough review and framework is established in the healthcare sector to determine legal ownership and resolve ethical issues related to data use. Data sovereignty, ethical integrity, and regulatory compliance in data management processes can be ensured by healthcare institutions by establishing strong frameworks and prioritizing patient rights.

THE EMERGENCE OF BLOCKCHAIN TECHNOLOGY IN INDIAN HEALTHCARE INDUSTRY

During the last decade, India has become part of the global economy, bringing economic prosperity. India has established itself as a major global partner. It has also seen significant progress in reducing poverty. From 2011 to 2020, more than 100 million people saw improvement in their living conditions, which helped them escape from poverty.

Essential factors like rising health awareness, income levels, lifestyle-related diseases, and better access to health insurance have increased the value of the Indian healthcare industry. It is estimated to reach over US$500 billion by 2023 end. In 2022, the Indian healthcare industry became one of the major employers, with a workforce of 5.1 million. Between 2020 and 2025, the healthcare industry is projected to create around 4 million jobs, with an average of 550,000 new job positions yearly.

Fig. (3) shows the barriers in the Indian medical sector. The emergence of IT and the rise of remote patient monitoring systems (RPM) has expanded digital health care worldwide. Remote patient monitoring has enhanced clinical assessment and decision-making and reduced the potential for hospitalization. The use of RPM services is expected to increase with the continuous rise in cancer, diabetes, and cardiovascular diseases. Moreover, remote patient monitoring systems are likely to increase with the rise in the elderly population and people choosing self-reliant and health-conscious lifestyles. The Indian healthcare system encounters fundamental obstacles linked to data that hinder effective health information administration and use.

Primary barriers in the Indian medical sector.

CONCLUSION

Healthcare might be transformed by blockchain technology. Healthcare stakeholders must collaborate more to gain from this innovation. Implementing blockchain-based technology in the medical sector may successfully handle several concerns, such as care coordination, data security, and interoperability issues. As technology advances, it will tackle technical obstacles like slow processing speed and extensive data replication. Healthcare data management has garnered increasing interest over the past decade due to its ability to provide more accurate, streamlined, and cost-efficient medical treatment. Technology can significantly improve medical data management by addressing problems often seen in current client-server and cloud-based methods, such as single-point collapse, fragility, dispersed data, and privacy risks. The possibility and relevance of blockchain technology and its trade-offs are elucidated via a comparative comparison. Several challenges must be comprehensively resolved to establish effective services that can fully use the advantages of blockchain-based technology.

Blockchain technology is mainly used in the medical sector to effectively disseminate and exchange significant information and medical data among specialists and professionals involved in research and development. The exponential growth of technology in healthcare institutions can provide new pathways for furthering medical research. The neural control scheme utilizes the blockchain scheme, and the technology may be stored in the digital brain during the observation phase. Incorporating sophisticated technology into the healthcare system is crucial for harnessing the full potential of blockchain in healthcare. The scope of this study includes clinical trials, system monitoring, health insurance, and medication tracking. Hospitals may use a Blockchain architecture to map their services by monitoring equipment during their entire life cycle. Utilizing a blockchain development business may enhance the management of patient history, particularly in the areas of tracking and insurance mediation. This can result in faster clinical interventions and improved data preservation. This technology has the potential to greatly benefit patients and clinicians by revolutionizing the use and management of clinical records, leading to improved healthcare services.
